      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-el",
        "exact_quote": "For a nonempty family of finite walks, $$\\label{eq:el-traffic-identity}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{EL}}_{\\mathsf G}(\\mathcal F)\n =\\inf_{\\mu}\\mathcal T_{\\mathsf G}(\\mu),$$ where finite-support probability laws on $\\mathcal F$ suffice for the infimum. General probability laws with finite expected edge counts give the same infimum.*\n\n\n*Let $A,B$ be nonempty disjoint vertex sets, and let $\\mathcal F_{\\mathsf G}(A,B)$ contain all walks in $\\mathsf G$ from $A$ to $B$. Then $$\\label{eq:port-resistance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{EL}}_{\\mathsf G}(\\mathcal F_{\\mathsf G}(A,B))\n =R_{\\mathrm{eff}}^{\\mathsf G}(A,B)\n =\\left[\n   \\inf_{\\substack{u|_A=0\\\\u|_B=1}}\\mathcal E_{\\mathsf G}^0(u)\n   \\right]^{-1}.$$ The two port sets are wired separately, and the resistance is computed in the same graph $\\mathsf G$ that confines the walks. If there is no connecting walk, the energy infimum is $0$ and its reciprocal is $\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-el",
          "exact_quote": "For a nonempty family of finite walks, $$\\label{eq:el-traffic-identity}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{EL}}_{\\mathsf G}(\\mathcal F)\n =\\inf_{\\mu}\\mathcal T_{\\mathsf G}(\\mu),$$ where finite-support probability laws on $\\mathcal F$ suffice for the infimum. General probability laws with finite expected edge counts give the same infimum.*\n\n\n*Let $A,B$ be nonempty disjoint vertex sets, and let $\\mathcal F_{\\mathsf G}(A,B)$ contain all walks in $\\mathsf G$ from $A$ to $B$. Then $$\\label{eq:port-resistance}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{EL}}_{\\mathsf G}(\\mathcal F_{\\mathsf G}(A,B))\n =R_{\\mathrm{eff}}^{\\mathsf G}(A,B)\n =\\left[\n   \\inf_{\\substack{u|_A=0\\\\u|_B=1}}\\mathcal E_{\\mathsf G}^0(u)\n   \\right]^{-1}.$$ The two port sets are wired separately, and the resistance is computed in the same graph $\\mathsf G$ that confines the walks. If there is no connecting walk, the energy infimum is $0$ and its reciprocal is $\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }

General probability laws with finite expected edge counts give the same infimum.*\n\n\n*Let $A,B$ be nonempty disjoint vertex sets, and let $\\mathcal F_{\\mathsf G}(A,B)$ contain all walks in $\\mathsf G$ from $A$ to $B$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-network-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Consider either the primal or the dual network. Fix a countable collection of intrinsic instructions from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:readout-extension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:readout-extension\">6</a>. Suppose their activated spatial interpretations have the strict ports, confinements, and complete stars described below, compactly in an ordinary reference chart. The same assertions hold in the sphere patches covered by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:sphere-support\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:sphere-support\">7</a>. In every joint representation extraction, almost surely on activation, each fixed instruction has the following properties for all sufficiently large discrete indices.*\n\n1.  *For the annular template <a href=\"#eq:annular-template\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:annular-template\">[eq:annular-template]</a>, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{EL}}\\bigl(\\mathcal A_m(z,r;\\mathbf a)\\bigr)\\ge c_{\\mathbf a}>0.$$ The constant is deterministic and depends only on the relative radii and the fixed protection margins.*\n\n2.  *Fix $0<a<b$, with a protected disk buffer around $B(z,br)$. There is a function $u_m$ on the network vertices with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ at every vertex whose projected complete flag star meets $\\overline B(z,ar)$, and equal to $0$ at every vertex whose projected star meets the exterior of $B(z,br)$. Its nonzero edge differences are supported in a fixed compact subannulus of $A(z;ar,br)$, and $$\\mathcal E^0(u_m)\\le C.$$ The constant depends only on $a,b$ and the chosen relative margins. In a protected patch the function is extended by the indicated constants across its two sides.*\n\n3.  *Fix $0<a<b<c$. There is a probability law on simple circuits $\\Gamma_m$ in the network, compactly confined in $A(z;br,cr)$ and surrounding $\\overline B(z,ar)$, such that $$\\sum_e\\mathbb P(e\\in\\Gamma_m)^2\\le C_{a,b,c}.$$ A loop and a two-edge Jordan circuit are permitted simple circuits. The constant also records the fixed inner and outer collar margins.*\n\n4.  *Let $W$ be connected and open, and let $P_0,P_1$ be nonempty open subsets of $W$. For any fixed compact polygonal route in $W$ from $P_0$ to $P_1$, there is a probability law $\\mu_m$ of graph paths between the vertex ports, compactly confined in $W$, with $$\\mathcal T(\\mu_m)\\le C<\\infty .$$ Here $C$ may depend on the route, its clearance from $W^c$, and its endpoint clearances inside the ports.*\n\n*For translates and dilates of one fixed relative template, the constants are the same; they do not depend on the absolute radius. The eventual index belongs to the fixed activated test and may depend on it and on the sample. The assertions hold simultaneously for the chosen countable collection.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },

There is a function $u_m$ on the network vertices with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ at every vertex whose projected complete flag star meets $\\overline B(z,ar)$, and equal to $0$ at every vertex whose projected star meets the exterior of $B(z,br)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-edge-density-budget",
        "exact_quote": "In the record-low extraction <a href=\"#eq:record-low-units\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:record-low-units\">[eq:record-low-units]</a>, let $U$ be a finite union of rational boxes compactly in an ordinary reference chart, and write $U^{+\\delta}$ for its Euclidean $\\delta$-neighborhood. Set $R_N=e^{-aN}$. Almost surely, for every sufficiently large $N$ from the aligned configuration there are nonnegative edge functions $g_{k,N,U}$, supported on edges whose entire images lie in $U^{+CR_N}$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\limsup_{k\\to\\infty}\\rho_{j_k}\\lVert g_{k,N,U}\\rVert_2^2\n     &\\le C\\,\\operatorname{Area}(U^{+CR_N}),\\label{eq:edge-budget-energy}\\\\\n L_{g_{k,N,U}}(\\pi;J)\n     &\\ge c\\,\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\Psi_{m_k}(\\pi(J))-CR_N-\\epsilon_{k,N,U}.\n       \\label{eq:edge-budget-charge}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\epsilon_{k,N,U}\\to0$ for fixed $N,U$, uniformly over every walk interval whose projected image is in $U$. The same density works for all such intervals and for every finite disjoint collection of them. The constants $c,C$ are deterministic and independent of $k,N,U$ once the shell geometry is fixed. The discrete threshold may depend on the finite configuration and its margins.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-edge-flow-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a sample on which the budgets of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:edge-density-budget\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:edge-density-budget\">12</a> hold for all sufficiently large members of a deterministic sequence of $N$’s and the countable box library. Suppose $\\mu_k$ are laws of paths confined in one compact set $K$ in the chart, their projected endpoints are separated by at least $d_0>0$, and $$\\label{eq:cheap-edge-flow}\n \\mathcal T(\\mu_k)\\le C_0\\rho_{j_k}.$$ After reparameterization and a subsequence, their projected laws converge weakly on $C([0,1],K)$ to a probability law $\\mu$. Its paths retain endpoint separation $d_0$ and are rectifiable almost surely. Their average arclength measure $\\Lambda$, counted with multiplicity, satisfies $$\\label{eq:average-edge-arclength}\n \\Lambda(U)\\le C\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Area}(U)}$$ for every finite union of rational boxes compactly in the chart. Consequently $\\Lambda$ is nonzero, finite on $K$, and absolutely continuous with respect to planar area.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-relaxation",
        "exact_quote": "After a further joint extraction of countably many cell-constrained infima, $\\mathcal R^-=\\mathcal R^+$ for every continuous $f$ and every relatively compact open $U\\subset D$, simultaneously. Write $\\mathcal R(f,U)$ for the common value. It has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *If $w_m$ converges locally uniformly to $f$ on $U$, in the vertex positions $z_m$, then $$\\mathcal R(f,U)\\le\\liminf_m\\mathcal E_m(w_m;U).$$*\n\n2.  *If $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$ and $\\mathcal R(f,U)<\\infty$, there are $w_m$ converging uniformly to $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$ such that $$\\limsup_m\\mathcal E_m(w_m;W)\\le\\mathcal R(f,U).$$*\n\n3.  *If $f_j\\in C(U)$ and $f_j\\to f$ uniformly on $W$, where $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$, then $$\\mathcal R(f,V)\\le\\liminf_j\\mathcal R(f_j,W).$$*\n\n*The functional is determined by the retained local numerical outputs on arbitrarily slightly larger domains. Two protected descriptions give the same functional on their common interior.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-relaxation",
          "exact_quote": "After a further joint extraction of countably many cell-constrained infima, $\\mathcal R^-=\\mathcal R^+$ for every continuous $f$ and every relatively compact open $U\\subset D$, simultaneously.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-conductivity",
        "exact_quote": "From every joint geometric extraction one can further extract the local numerical outputs so that the following holds almost surely. For the primal and the unit dual network, for every open $U$ compactly contained in an ordinary conformal chart, and every $f\\in C(U)$, $$\\label{eq:unit-conductivity}\n \\mathcal R(f,U)=\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx.$$ The right side is $+\\infty$ if $f\\notin H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$, and is allowed to be infinite otherwise. For relaxations obtained along extractions of finite spherical maps, the same identity holds in the common canonical coordinate on every coordinate domain; the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral.*\n\n*In particular, every sequence converging locally uniformly to $f$ has the lower energy bound of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-relaxation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-relaxation\">15</a>(i). For $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$ with finite right side in <a href=\"#eq:unit-conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:unit-conductivity\">[eq:unit-conductivity]</a>, there is a sequence of vertex functions $w_m$ converging uniformly to $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$, with limsup energy on $W$ at most that right side. The coefficient in <a href=\"#eq:unit-conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:unit-conductivity\">[eq:unit-conductivity]</a> is the same for all extractions.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-conductivity",
          "exact_quote": "From every joint geometric extraction one can further extract the local numerical outputs so that the following holds almost surely. For relaxations obtained along extractions of finite spherical maps, the same identity holds in the common canonical coordinate on every coordinate domain; the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral.*\n\n*In particular, every sequence converging locally uniformly to $f$ has the lower energy bound of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-relaxation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-relaxation\">15</a>(i). For $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$ with finite right side in <a href=\"#eq:unit-conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:unit-conductivity\">[eq:unit-conductivity]</a>, there is a sequence of vertex functions $w_m$ converging uniformly to $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$, with limsup energy on $W$ at most that right side. The coefficient in <a href=\"#eq:unit-conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:unit-conductivity\">[eq:unit-conductivity]</a> is the same for all extractions.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "From every joint geometric extraction one can further extract the local numerical outputs so that the following holds almost surely. For relaxations obtained along extractions of finite spherical maps, the same identity holds in the common canonical coordinate on every coordinate domain; the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral.*\n\n*In particular, every sequence converging locally uniformly to $f$ has the lower energy bound of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-relaxation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-relaxation\">15</a>(i). For $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$ with finite right side in <a href=\"#eq:unit-conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:unit-conductivity\">[eq:unit-conductivity]</a>, there is a sequence of vertex functions $w_m$ converging uniformly to $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$, with limsup energy on $W$ at most that right side.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-measures",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f\\in C(U)$ and $\\mathcal R(f,V)<\\infty$ for every $V\\Subset U$. The values $V\\mapsto\\mathcal R(f,V)$ are the open-set values of a Radon measure $\\Gamma_f$ on $U$. These measures are local: if $f=g$ on an open set, their restrictions there agree. They are invariant under addition of constants. For locally finite values they satisfy $$\\label{eq:measure-parallelogram}\n \\Gamma_{f+g}+\\Gamma_{f-g}=2\\Gamma_f+2\\Gamma_g,\n \\qquad \\Gamma_{af}=a^2\\Gamma_f\\quad(a\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Consequently $$\\Gamma(f,g)=\\tfrac14(\\Gamma_{f+g}-\\Gamma_{f-g})$$ is a symmetric bilinear signed Radon measure. For every relatively compact Borel set $E$, $$\\label{eq:measure-cauchy-schwarz}\n \\lvert \\Gamma(f,g)(E)\\rvert^2\\le\\Gamma_f(E)\\Gamma_g(E).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-measures",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $f\\in C(U)$ and $\\mathcal R(f,V)<\\infty$ for every $V\\Subset U$. The values $V\\mapsto\\mathcal R(f,V)$ are the open-set values of a Radon measure $\\Gamma_f$ on $U$. These measures are local: if $f=g$ on an open set, their restrictions there agree. They are invariant under addition of constants. For locally finite values they satisfy $$\\label{eq:measure-parallelogram}\n \\Gamma_{f+g}+\\Gamma_{f-g}=2\\Gamma_f+2\\Gamma_g,\n \\qquad \\Gamma_{af}=a^2\\Gamma_f\\quad(a\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Consequently $$\\Gamma(f,g)=\\tfrac14(\\Gamma_{f+g}-\\Gamma_{f-g})$$ is a symmetric bilinear signed Radon measure. For every relatively compact Borel set $E$, $$\\label{eq:measure-cauchy-schwarz}\n \\lvert \\Gamma(f,g)(E)\\rvert^2\\le\\Gamma_f(E)\\Gamma_g(E).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $f\\in C(U)$ and $\\mathcal R(f,V)<\\infty$ for every $V\\Subset U$. For locally finite values they satisfy $$\\label{eq:measure-parallelogram}\n \\Gamma_{f+g}+\\Gamma_{f-g}=2\\Gamma_f+2\\Gamma_g,\n \\qquad \\Gamma_{af}=a^2\\Gamma_f\\quad(a\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Consequently $$\\Gamma(f,g)=\\tfrac14(\\Gamma_{f+g}-\\Gamma_{f-g})$$ is a symmetric bilinear signed Radon measure. For every relatively compact Borel set $E$, $$\\label{eq:measure-cauchy-schwarz}\n \\lvert \\Gamma(f,g)(E)\\rvert^2\\le\\Gamma_f(E)\\Gamma_g(E).$$",
      "explanation": "The relaxation defines local Radon energy measures that are quadratic and satisfy Cauchy-Schwarz.",

      "id": "claim-energy-measures",

          "exact_quote": "for every $V\\Subset U$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"

      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-measures"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For every $D_0\\Subset D$ there are deterministic constants $0<c_{D_0}\\le C_{D_0}<\\infty$ such that, for every open $U\\Subset D_0$, $$\\label{eq:energy-comparison}\n c_{D_0}\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx\n \\le \\mathcal R(f,U)\n \\le C_{D_0}\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx.$$ For a continuous $f$ outside $H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$, the lower integral is interpreted as $+\\infty$. The upper bound first holds for smooth $f$, and holds also for $f\\in C(U)\\cap H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. Local finiteness of $\\mathcal R(f,\\cdot)$ for a continuous $f$ implies $f\\in H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. The inequalities also hold as inequalities of measures on every subopen of $D_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "For every $D_0\\Subset D$ there are deterministic constants $0<c_{D_0}\\le C_{D_0}<\\infty$ such that, for every open $U\\Subset D_0$, $$\\label{eq:energy-comparison}\n c_{D_0}\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx\n \\le \\mathcal R(f,U)\n \\le C_{D_0}\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx.$$ For a continuous $f$ outside $H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$, the lower integral is interpreted as $+\\infty$. The upper bound first holds for smooth $f$, and holds also for $f\\in C(U)\\cap H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. Local finiteness of $\\mathcal R(f,\\cdot)$ for a continuous $f$ implies $f\\in H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. The upper bound first holds for smooth $f$, and holds also for $f\\in C(U)\\cap H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. Local finiteness of $\\mathcal R(f,\\cdot)$ for a continuous $f$ implies $f\\in H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)$. The inequalities also hold as inequalities of measures on every subopen of $D_0$.",
      "explanation": "The relaxed energy is comparable to the Sobolev energy with deterministic constants.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-tensor",
        "exact_quote": "For each network, in each joint extraction there is a deterministic constant $k>0$, possibly depending on the extraction and on the choice of primal or dual, such that in every ordinary chart $$\\label{eq:scalar-relaxation}\n \\mathcal R(f,U)=k\\int_U\\lvert \\nabla f\\rvert^2\\,\\mathrm dx\n \\qquad(f\\in C(U)\\cap H^1_{\\rm loc}(U)).$$ The formula is understood with infinite values on an exhaustion. The corresponding probability-one local assertion transfers to strict-interior sphere charts with the same constant and the same raw energy units.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite connected planar multigraph in a closed disk whose boundary is a simple cycle. Split that cycle into four nonempty edge paths $D_0,N_0,D_1,N_1$ in cyclic order, with four distinct joining vertices. Give every edge a conductance $c_e>0$. Let $$C_{\\rm p}\n =\\min\\left\\{\\sum_{e\\in E(G)}c_e(d_e u)^2:\n             u=0\\text{ on }D_0,\\quad u=1\\text{ on }D_1\\right\\}.$$ The endpoints of each prescribed arc are included in its constraint.*\n\n*Form a dual graph with one vertex for each interior face and two additional vertices $b_0,b_1$. Each interior edge has its usual dual, with conductance $c_e^{-1}$. A boundary edge on $N_i$ has a dual edge from its interior face to $b_i$, with conductance $c_e^{-1}$. Discard duals of boundary edges on $D_0,D_1$. Let $C_{\\rm d}$ be the minimum dual energy with values $0,1$ at $b_0,b_1$. Then $$\\label{eq:finite-duality}\n                              C_{\\rm p}C_{\\rm d}=1.$$ The statement permits loops, parallel edges, and repeated face incidences.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite connected planar multigraph in a closed disk whose boundary is a simple cycle. Each interior edge has its usual dual, with conductance $c_e^{-1}$. A boundary edge on $N_i$ has a dual edge from its interior face to $b_i$, with conductance $c_e^{-1}$. Discard duals of boundary edges on $D_0,D_1$. Let $C_{\\rm d}$ be the minimum dual energy with values $0,1$ at $b_0,b_1$. Then $$\\label{eq:finite-duality}\n                              C_{\\rm p}C_{\\rm d}=1.$$ The statement permits loops, parallel edges, and repeated face incidences.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-harmonic-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O$ be an open coordinate region in the extracted networks. Suppose $w_m$ is harmonic at every vertex with full star in $O$, and for every $V\\Subset O$, $\\sup_m\\mathcal E_m(w_m;V)<\\infty$. Then $w_m$ is asymptotically equicontinuous on compact subsets of $O$: if $z_m(v_m),z_m(v_m')$ lie in a fixed compact subset and their distance tends to zero, then $\\lvert w_m(v_m)-w_m(v_m')\\rvert\\to0$. If the values are also locally bounded, every subsequence has a further subsequence converging locally uniformly in vertex tests to a continuous function.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-square-conductance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q=(0,1)^2$ have closure in an ordinary canonical chart.       },

        "exact_quote": "Fix a joint extraction on which Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometry\">5</a>, Theorem <a href=\"#thm:network-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:network-bounds\">11</a>, and Theorem <a href=\"#thm:conductivity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:conductivity\">16</a> hold. Let $\\mathsf a_n,\\mathsf b_n,\\mathsf c_n,\\mathsf d_n$ be vertex sequences whose canonical positions converge respectively to four pairwise distinct points $x,y,z,z_0$. Normalize the solution of <a href=\"#eq:point-current\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:point-current\">[eq:point-current]</a> by $u_n(\\mathsf d_n)=0$. Then, for every compact $K\\subset\\mathbb S^2\\setminus\\{x,y\\}$, $$\\label{eq:local-voltage-convergence}\n \\sup_{v:\\,z_n(v)\\in K}\n    \\lvert u_n(v)-U_{x,y;z_0}(z_n(v))\\rvert\\longrightarrow0.$$ An empty supremum in <a href=\"#eq:local-voltage-convergence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:local-voltage-convergence\">[eq:local-voltage-convergence]</a> is assigned the value zero. In particular, $$u_n(\\mathsf c_n)-u_n(\\mathsf d_n)\\longrightarrow\n \\frac1{2\\pi}\n       \\log\\frac{\\operatorname{ch}(z,y)\\operatorname{ch}(z_0,x)}\n                     {\\operatorname{ch}(z,x)\\operatorname{ch}(z_0,y)}.$$ The assertion holds for any such varying vertex sequences on the extracted sample.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-block-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "From an arbitrary sequence of finite spheres, take a further joint representation extraction as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometry\">5</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:readout-extension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:readout-extension\">6</a>, retaining the interval readouts defined below. Put $m=2n$ and $p=7/10$. Simultaneously for the countable protected interval atlas, the following holds almost surely on activation of an entry $(I_m,G_m^+)$. There are finite sample-dependent constants $j_*$, $C$, and $n_*$ such that, at every index $n\\ge n_*$ in the extraction, for every function on $V(G_m^+)$ and every integer $j\\ge j_*$, $$\\label{eq:block-approximation}\n \\frac1m\\sum_{i\\in I_m}\n   \\lvert f(v(i))-(P_j^{I_m}f)(i)\\rvert^2\n \\le C\\,2^{-pj} \\mathcal E_n^0(f;G_m^+).$$ This includes all levels down to and beyond singleton cuts and holds for all functions on the same event.*\n\n*In the two-rooting extraction of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometry\">5</a>, one can choose finitely many activated entries from the two rooted atlases such that every corner occurrence belongs to at least one of their intervals, in its corresponding rooted order, for all sufficiently large $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-block-approximation",
          "exact_quote": "From an arbitrary sequence of finite spheres, take a further joint representation extraction as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometry\">5</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:readout-extension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:readout-extension\">6</a>, retaining the interval readouts defined below. Put $m=2n$ and $p=7/10$. Simultaneously for the countable protected interval atlas, the following holds almost surely on activation of an entry $(I_m,G_m^+)$. There are finite sample-dependent constants $j_*$, $C$, and $n_*$ such that, at every index $n\\ge n_*$ in the extraction, for every function on $V(G_m^+)$ and every integer $j\\ge j_*$, $$\\label{eq:block-approximation}\n \\frac1m\\sum_{i\\in I_m}\n   \\lvert f(v(i))-(P_j^{I_m}f)(i)\\rvert^2\n \\le C\\,2^{-pj} \\mathcal E_n^0(f;G_m^+).$$ This includes all levels down to and beyond singleton cuts and holds for all functions on the same event.*\n\n*In the two-rooting extraction of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometry\">5</a>, one can choose finitely many activated entries from the two rooted atlases such that every corner occurrence belongs to at least one of their intervals, in its corresponding rooted order, for all sufficiently large $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-address-input",
        "exact_quote": "For the marker systems just described, with fixed $b,R,K$, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *If $c_k$ is the number of components of the union of the two colored graphs, then $$c_k\\le b_k+D_k,\\qquad e_k\\ge m_k-c_k.$$ Moreover $$\\mathbb E_\\Xi\\sum_kD_k\\le C_{K,R}M_{\\rm jump}.$$ If the bases lie in an interval of length $C r_m(N)$, then $\\mathbb E_\\Xi\\sum_{k\\le N/\\ell_R}(b_k-1)\\le C_{C,R}$.*\n\n\n2.  *For each $\\varepsilon>0$, a fixed sufficiently large $R$ gives, with conditional grid probability at least $7/8$, disjoint regular sampling slots after deletion of at most $C_RM_{\\rm term}+\\varepsilon T_{\\rm occ}$ node occurrences. For each sufficiently small fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, $R$ and the deletion tolerances may be fixed so that every such code $\\mathfrak c$ obeys $$\\label{eq:fixed-code-charge}\n     \\mathbb P_m^W\\{\\mathfrak c\\text{ is realized}\\}\n     \\le\n     \\exp\\{-\\vartheta\\ell_R R_{\\rm rk}\n           +\\varepsilon\\ell_R T_{\\rm occ}\n           +C_{R,\\varepsilon}M_{\\rm term}\\}.$$ Realization includes all its actual chord constraints. The bound is uniform in the fixed grid displacement and in the exact chord endpoints inside the recorded cells, once the minimum sampling radius diverges.*\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed displacement and fixed augmented distinct probe depths, and every $0<\\eta\\le K$, the number of decorated codes with occupancy $T_{\\rm occ}=t$ is at most the exponential of $$\\label{eq:address-count}\n     (\\ell_R+C_0+\\eta)t\n     +K P_N\\log(K/\\eta)+C P_NK\\log(K+1)+O(1).$$ If the depth list itself is encoded, add $\\log\\binom{J_R+1}{P_N}$; our independent mesh phases are instead held as side information.*\n\n4.  *Let $\\Delta(\\Xi)$ count generations at which the partition of the active markers into shifted cells differs from the grid-independent partition obtained by joining consecutive active positions with gap $<\\delta_k$. Then $$\\mathbb E_\\Xi[\\Delta(\\Xi)\\mid\\text{markers}]\n     \\le \\frac{2R}{R-1}M_{\\rm term}.$$ At a generation with equal partitions the entire colored graph and its base labels agree up to cell names.*\n\n*For $P_N=O(N/L)$, take the error tolerances and $R$ fixed first, then $N$ and the minimum units to infinity with $L\\to\\infty$ and $L=o(N)$. The terminal, jump, and structural errors above are $o(N)$ for every fixed $K$. One may subsequently let $K=K_m\\to\\infty$ sufficiently slowly, with $$\\frac{K_m\\log(K_mL)}{L}\\longrightarrow0,\\qquad\n \\frac{K_m\\log(2\\widehat C_m)}{L}\\longrightarrow0,$$ where $2\\widehat C_m m$ is a growing enclosure length, and with the composed exploration depth below $D_m^{\\max}$. The second condition pays for its extra address ancestors. The integrated jump error remains $o(N)$, and each previously fixed minimum-unit threshold is retained.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-truncated-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Use the selected protocol above with a fixed number of bases. Let $\\mathcal I_N\\subset[a_-N,bN]$, with fixed $a_->1$, be a deterministic log interval of length at least $cN>0$, and let $Y$ be uniform on $\\mathcal I_N$, independently after the selected environment. Then there is $C_{\\rm pack}<\\infty$, independent of each fixed $k,M$, such that $$\\label{eq:truncated-packing}\n \\sup_{k,M\\ge1}\\ \n \\lim_{H\\to\\infty}\\limsup_m\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathbb Q_m}\\min\\{\\Pi_{m,k,H}(Y),M\\}\n \\le C_{\\rm pack}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reachable-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a_0>1$, $c_0,C_0,\\zeta>0$, and a finite integer $d$. Under the selected protocol with $r_m(a_0N)\\to\\infty$, there is no sequence of selected laws supported on the existence of more than $e^{\\zeta N}$ times in the starting window, pairwise separated by at least $c_0r_m(N)$, with one common member from which every member is reached by at most $d$ scalar links or jumps of size at most $C_0r_m(a_0N)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-one-base-credit",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $B>2$ with the following property. Use a sparse address system from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:address-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:address-input\">27</a> with one base, upper range $BN$, and selected law <a href=\"#eq:selected-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:selected-density\">[eq:selected-density]</a>. Fix $1<a\\le2$ and any deterministic $\\eta_m\\downarrow0$. The probability that the projected component containing the base has a rank cycle at all but $\\eta_mN$ generations with log level $k\\ell_R\\in[aN,BN]$ tends to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-view-input",
        "exact_quote": "Use the selected protocol, the packing bound <a href=\"#eq:truncated-packing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:truncated-packing\">[eq:truncated-packing]</a>, and the sparse all-seed cutoffs of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:address-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:address-input\">27</a>. Fix $1<a_0<a<b$. Select and label the bases $1,\\ldots,h_{\\mathrm b}$, and fix a deterministic label $\\iota\\in\\{1,\\ldots,h_{\\mathrm b}\\}$, before the independent seed-generation mesh and address grids. The common all-seed trial array uses all distinguished bases and is the same for each fixed choice of $\\iota$; the conclusions below concern base $\\iota$. Use the finite-view construction with a uniform seed in $[aN,bN]$, freeze its centers, and then use its independent coarsening of log length $L_m\\to\\infty$. Its seed separation parameter $\\mathsf H_m\\to\\infty$ is chosen so that $L_m=o(N\\wedge\\log\\mathsf H_m)$. Write $X$ for the coarsened level. Removing an event of probability $o(1)$, $X\\in[aN,bN]$, and its density on that range is at most $C/N$. Retain the center graph and the acyclicity indicators of its base components in the common trial-array extraction, and follow the indicator for base $\\iota$; do not condition the view law after this sampling.*\n\n*After a further joint extraction, the following holds on that acyclic event, with the fixed truncations and subsequent removal of losses specified below. Write $r=r_m(X)$ and $c_{m,i}$ for the center origins. Two origins belong to the same scalar class of color $c$ when $$\\frac{Z^c(c_{m,i})-Z^c(c_{m,j})}{\\sqrt r}\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\liminf_m\\frac{\\min_{[c_{m,i}\\wedge c_{m,j},\\,c_{m,i}\\vee c_{m,j}]}\n Z^c-Z^c(c_{m,i})}{\\sqrt r}\\ge0.$$ The combined graph is the union of these two colored class graphs.*\n\n1.  *There are finitely many center lines in the base’s combined component, with tight count, and distinct origins diverge in $r_m(X)$ units. The base has a tight offset from its line origin in $r_m(X)$ units. Consecutive lines of each scalar class are joined by simultaneous actual scalar return links whose endpoints approach their origins in these units. For each such colored center pair, the preselected witness graph contains the same pair bit, with an actual seed witness whose endpoints have tight coordinates in seed units.*\n\n2.  *A planar disk and an annular collar in an auxiliary sphere agree with the original primal map on all their incidences and complete stars needed for separation. Their full time preimages lie in retained intervals on the component’s lines. The inner disk contains the base walk for a time radius $\\delta_m r_m(X)$, and the full preimage of the closed outer disk has total length at most $M_m r_m(X)$. Here $\\delta_m^{-1}$, $M_m$, and the number of lines are tight. The maximum absolute coordinate of every retained interval endpoint relative to its assigned line origin is tight in $r_m(X)$ units. For each fixed recipe truncation every retained slot and safety band has duration between fixed positive constants in view units.*\n\n3.  *The annular collar admits a recipe in a countable family. For a fixed recipe its input consists of retained off-center block paths, forest shifts, durations, class anchors, and fixed instructions. Feasibility is measurable from that input. Its recorded port sets and confinement graphs use exact retained incidences; every allowed path between its ports lies in the prescribed outer tube. The cyclic transverse tube pattern forces any collection of successful recorded crossings to contain a simple circuit in the original patch whose interior contains the inner disk and whose closed disk lies in the outer disk. The recipe has a positive-density ordinary sphere completion of fixed positive duration after a fixed duration truncation.*\n\n*All slots and guards used by the annular recipe avoid the distinguished center times. Its observation family is a countable family of fixed-recipe outputs chosen before the randomized class anchors; the anchors can enter feasibility and the later recipe selection.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-view-input",
          "exact_quote": "Use the selected protocol, the packing bound <a href=\"#eq:truncated-packing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:truncated-packing\">[eq:truncated-packing]</a>, and the sparse all-seed cutoffs of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:address-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:address-input\">27</a>. Fix $1<a_0<a<b$. Select and label the bases $1,\\ldots,h_{\\mathrm b}$, and fix a deterministic label $\\iota\\in\\{1,\\ldots,h_{\\mathrm b}\\}$, before the independent seed-generation mesh and address grids. The common all-seed trial array uses all distinguished bases and is the same for each fixed choice of $\\iota$; the conclusions below concern base $\\iota$. Use the finite-view construction with a uniform seed in $[aN,bN]$, freeze its centers, and then use its independent coarsening of log length $L_m\\to\\infty$. Its seed separation parameter $\\mathsf H_m\\to\\infty$ is chosen so that $L_m=o(N\\wedge\\log\\mathsf H_m)$. Write $X$ for the coarsened level. Removing an event of probability $o(1)$, $X\\in[aN,bN]$, and its density on that range is at most $C/N$. Retain the center graph and the acyclicity indicators of its base components in the common trial-array extraction, and follow the indicator for base $\\iota$; do not condition the view law after this sampling.*\n\n*After a further joint extraction, the following holds on that acyclic event, with the fixed truncations and subsequent removal of losses specified below. Write $r=r_m(X)$ and $c_{m,i}$ for the center origins. Two origins belong to the same scalar class of color $c$ when $$\\frac{Z^c(c_{m,i})-Z^c(c_{m,j})}{\\sqrt r}\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\liminf_m\\frac{\\min_{[c_{m,i}\\wedge c_{m,j},\\,c_{m,i}\\vee c_{m,j}]}\n Z^c-Z^c(c_{m,i})}{\\sqrt r}\\ge0.$$ The combined graph is the union of these two colored class graphs.*\n\n1.  *There are finitely many center lines in the base’s combined component, with tight count, and distinct origins diverge in $r_m(X)$ units. The base has a tight offset from its line origin in $r_m(X)$ units. Consecutive lines of each scalar class are joined by simultaneous actual scalar return links whose endpoints approach their origins in these units. For each such colored center pair, the preselected witness graph contains the same pair bit, with an actual seed witness whose endpoints have tight coordinates in seed units.*\n\n2.  *A planar disk and an annular collar in an auxiliary sphere agree with the original primal map on all their incidences and complete stars needed for separation. Their full time preimages lie in retained intervals on the component’s lines. The inner disk contains the base walk for a time radius $\\delta_m r_m(X)$, and the full preimage of the closed outer disk has total length at most $M_m r_m(X)$. Here $\\delta_m^{-1}$, $M_m$, and the number of lines are tight. The maximum absolute coordinate of every retained interval endpoint relative to its assigned line origin is tight in $r_m(X)$ units. For each fixed recipe truncation every retained slot and safety band has duration between fixed positive constants in view units.*\n\n3.  *The annular collar admits a recipe in a countable family. For a fixed recipe its input consists of retained off-center block paths, forest shifts, durations, class anchors, and fixed instructions. Feasibility is measurable from that input. Its recorded port sets and confinement graphs use exact retained incidences; every allowed path between its ports lies in the prescribed outer tube. The cyclic transverse tube pattern forces any collection of successful recorded crossings to contain a simple circuit in the original patch whose interior contains the inner disk and whose closed disk lies in the outer disk. The recipe has a positive-density ordinary sphere completion of fixed positive duration after a fixed duration truncation.*\n\n*All slots and guards used by the annular recipe avoid the distinguished center times. Its observation family is a countable family of fixed-recipe outputs chosen before the randomized class anchors; the anchors can enter feasibility and the later recipe selection.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-flow-availability",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\zeta>0$, a tested chronological window, and an open planar confinement $U$ with the preceding fixed-window and positive-clearance cutoffs. In the preceding definition of availability take $D=D_m$ and take $d_m,\\ell_m$ to be the integer-rounded values in <a href=\"#eq:available-traffic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:available-traffic\">[eq:available-traffic]</a>. Let $D_m\\to\\infty$ be deterministic and below the preselection depth bound, with any finite-view cutoffs slowed below it. Under <a href=\"#eq:selected-units\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:selected-units\">[eq:selected-units]</a>, there is no sequence of selected laws supported on $h_{\\mathrm b}$ unavailable times, pairwise separated by $\\ell_m$, in an interval of length at most $C r_m(N)$, in either of these fixed parameter regimes:*\n\n1.  *$1<a_0<a<b$, with $a-1$ sufficiently small relative to $(\\vartheta-1)(b-a)$ and $h_{\\mathrm b}$ sufficiently large as in (OpenAI 2026b, Proposition 10.18) [[P](#ref-P)];*\n\n2.  *$1<a_0<a\\le2$, $b=B$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:one-base-credit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:one-base-credit\">30</a>, and $h_{\\mathrm b}=1$.*\n\n*The same statement holds for every fixed multiple of the interval length and every fixed positive confinement clearance.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-overlap",
        "exact_quote": "At every such level with uniformly diverging view units, the common event above can be chosen so that, for every family of disjoint blocks and every vertex function, $$\\label{eq:energy-overlap}\n \\sum_I\\mathcal E_m(f;W_I)\n \\le C e^{N/40}\\mathcal E_m(f;G_m^+).$$ At levels where only chronological edges are used, the same statement holds with a constant in place of $Ce^{N/40}$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-segment-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge1$, $\\ell\\ge4d$, and $h_{\\mathrm b}\\ge1$ be integers. An integer $t$ is called good if it has a simple circuit in $U$ with $\\operatorname{var}_f(C)\\le L$, whose open interior contains $v([t-d,t+d])$, and whose closed disk contains no run of $\\ell$ steps in the tested window. There is a constant $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}$, depending only on $h_{\\mathrm b}$, with the following property. Suppose an interval $I$ of at most $T$ steps has no $h_{\\mathrm b}$ pairwise $\\ell$-separated bad times, and the walk on a $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}\\ell$ enlargement stays in $U$, with that enlargement inside the tested window. Then:*\n\n1.  *At most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}(1+T/d)$ good circuits cover the walk on $I$ by their open interiors except for at most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}$ chronological intervals, each of length at most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}\\ell$.*\n\n2.  *There are graph paths in $U$ with total $\\lvert df\\rvert$-cost at most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}(1+T/d)L$ which, together with at most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}$ unresolved endpoint pairs separated by at most $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}\\ell$, join the endpoints of the walk on $I$. All unresolved times lie in that enlargement.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-segment-cover",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge1$, $\\ell\\ge4d$, and $h_{\\mathrm b}\\ge1$ be integers. There is a constant $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}$, depending only on $h_{\\mathrm b}$, with the following property. Suppose an interval $I$ of at most $T$ steps has no $h_{\\mathrm b}$ pairwise $\\ell$-separated bad times, and the walk on a $K_{h_{\\mathrm b}}\\ell$ enlargement stays in $U$, with that enlargement inside the tested window.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-green",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a Borel probability measure on the round sphere. Suppose that, for constants $a>0$, $b<\\infty$, $c,C>0$, and $r_*>0$, $$\\label{eq:deterministic-ball-bounds}\n c r^b\\le\\mu(B_{d_0}(x,r))\\le C r^a\n \\quad(x\\in\\mathbb S^2, 0<r\\le r_*).$$ Smooth restriction extends uniquely to a bounded injective map $R_\\mu:H^1(\\mathbb S^2,\\mathrm{vol}_0)\\longrightarrow L^2(\\mu)$. The measure $\\mu$ charges no $H^1$-polar set, and $R_\\mu f$ is the quasi-continuous representative of $f$, $\\mu$-almost everywhere. There is a constant $C_\\mu<\\infty$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|R_\\mu(f-\\sigma_0(f))\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n       &\\le C_\\mu\\mathcal E^0(f)^{1/2},\\label{eq:centered-trace}\\\\\n C_\\mu^{-1}\\|f\\|_{H^1}\n       &\\le \\bigl(\\|R_\\mu f\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}^2+\\mathcal E^0(f)\\bigr)^{1/2}\n       \\le C_\\mu\\|f\\|_{H^1}.\\label{eq:trace-norm-equivalence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, for each $g\\in L^2(\\mu)$ there is a unique $u_g\\in H^1$ with $\\sigma_0(u_g)=0$ such that $$\\label{eq:potential-weak-equation}\n \\mathcal E^0(u_g,f)=\\int g R_\\mu f\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\n               -\\left(\\int g\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\\right)\\sigma_0(f)\n \\quad(f\\in H^1).$$ The integral operator $T_\\mu$ with kernel $G$ is a Hilbert–Schmidt operator on $L^2(\\mu)$, and the potential has the trace $$\\label{eq:potential-trace}\n R_\\mu u_g=T_\\mu g,\\qquad\n T_\\mu g(x)=\\int G(x,y)g(y)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(y)\n \\quad\\hbox{in }L^2(\\mu).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-green",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a Borel probability measure on the round sphere. Suppose that, for constants $a>0$, $b<\\infty$, $c,C>0$, and $r_*>0$, $$\\label{eq:deterministic-ball-bounds}\n c r^b\\le\\mu(B_{d_0}(x,r))\\le C r^a\n \\quad(x\\in\\mathbb S^2, 0<r\\le r_*).$$ Smooth restriction extends uniquely to a bounded injective map $R_\\mu:H^1(\\mathbb S^2,\\mathrm{vol}_0)\\longrightarrow L^2(\\mu)$. The measure $\\mu$ charges no $H^1$-polar set, and $R_\\mu f$ is the quasi-continuous representative of $f$, $\\mu$-almost everywhere. There is a constant $C_\\mu<\\infty$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|R_\\mu(f-\\sigma_0(f))\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n       &\\le C_\\mu\\mathcal E^0(f)^{1/2},\\label{eq:centered-trace}\\\\\n C_\\mu^{-1}\\|f\\|_{H^1}\n       &\\le \\bigl(\\|R_\\mu f\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}^2+\\mathcal E^0(f)\\bigr)^{1/2}\n       \\le C_\\mu\\|f\\|_{H^1}.\\label{eq:trace-norm-equivalence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, for each $g\\in L^2(\\mu)$ there is a unique $u_g\\in H^1$ with $\\sigma_0(u_g)=0$ such that $$\\label{eq:potential-weak-equation}\n \\mathcal E^0(u_g,f)=\\int g R_\\mu f\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\n               -\\left(\\int g\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\\right)\\sigma_0(f)\n \\quad(f\\in H^1).$$ The integral operator $T_\\mu$ with kernel $G$ is a Hilbert–Schmidt operator on $L^2(\\mu)$, and the potential has the trace $$\\label{eq:potential-trace}\n R_\\mu u_g=T_\\mu g,\\qquad\n T_\\mu g(x)=\\int G(x,y)g(y)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(y)\n \\quad\\hbox{in }L^2(\\mu).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mu$ be a Borel probability measure on the round sphere. Suppose that, for constants $a>0$, $b<\\infty$, $c,C>0$, and $r_*>0$, $$\\label{eq:deterministic-ball-bounds}\n c r^b\\le\\mu(B_{d_0}(x,r))\\le C r^a\n \\quad(x\\in\\mathbb S^2, 0<r\\le r_*).$$ Smooth restriction extends uniquely to a bounded injective map $R_\\mu:H^1(\\mathbb S^2,\\mathrm{vol}_0)\\longrightarrow L^2(\\mu)$. The measure $\\mu$ charges no $H^1$-polar set, and $R_\\mu f$ is the quasi-continuous representative of $f$, $\\mu$-almost everywhere. There is a constant $C_\\mu<\\infty$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|R_\\mu(f-\\sigma_0(f))\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n       &\\le C_\\mu\\mathcal E^0(f)^{1/2},\\label{eq:centered-trace}\\\\\n C_\\mu^{-1}\\|f\\|_{H^1}\n       &\\le \\bigl(\\|R_\\mu f\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}^2+\\mathcal E^0(f)\\bigr)^{1/2}\n       \\le C_\\mu\\|f\\|_{H^1}.\\label{eq:trace-norm-equivalence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, for each $g\\in L^2(\\mu)$ there is a unique $u_g\\in H^1$ with $\\sigma_0(u_g)=0$ such that $$\\label{eq:potential-weak-equation}\n \\mathcal E^0(u_g,f)=\\int g R_\\mu f\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\n               -\\left(\\int g\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\\right)\\sigma_0(f)\n \\quad(f\\in H^1).$$ The integral operator $T_\\mu$ with kernel $G$ is a Hilbert–Schmidt operator on $L^2(\\mu)$, and the potential has the trace $$\\label{eq:potential-trace}\n R_\\mu u_g=T_\\mu g,\\qquad\n T_\\mu g(x)=\\int G(x,y)g(y)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(y)\n \\quad\\hbox{in }L^2(\\mu).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-continuum-form",
        "exact_quote": "Almost surely, the closure in $L^2(\\mu_h)$ of the smooth form $\\frac12\\mathcal E^0$ has domain $$\\label{eq:continuum-form-domain}\n \\mathcal D_h=R_{\\mu_h}(H^1(\\mathbb S^2,\\mathrm{vol}_0)),\\qquad\n \\mathcal E_h(R_{\\mu_h}f,R_{\\mu_h}g)=\\frac12\\mathcal E^0(f,g).$$ It is a densely defined closed regular Dirichlet form. Let $A_h$ be its nonnegative self-adjoint operator, and set $B_h=2A_h$, the operator of the raw form $\\mathcal E^0$. Put $\\mu=\\mu_h$ and define $$\\begin{aligned}\n G_\\mu(x,y)={}&G(x,y)-\\int G(x,z)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(z)-\\int G(z,y)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(z)\n       +\\iint G(z,w)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(z)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(w),\\label{eq:double-centered-green}\\\\\n K_hg(x)={}&\\int G_\\mu(x,y)g(y)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(y).\n       \\label{eq:continuum-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The kernel $G_\\mu$ is in $L^2(\\mu\\otimes\\mu)$ and has zero $\\mu$-mean in each variable. The operator $K_h$ is zero on constants and is the positive, injective inverse of $B_h$ on $L^2_0(\\mu)$, the subspace of $\\mu$-mean-zero functions.*\n\n\n*Both $B_h$ and $A_h$ have compact resolvent. On the infinite-dimensional space $L^2(\\mu)$ their eigenvalues, counted with multiplicity, can be written $$0=\\nu_0<\\nu_1\\le\\nu_2\\le\\cdots\\longrightarrow\\infty,\n \\qquad \\Lambda_j=\\frac{\\nu_j}{2}\\quad(j\\ge0),$$ respectively. The constant eigenfunction is the only zero mode, and $$\\label{eq:continuum-inverse-summability}\n \\sum_{j\\ge1}\\nu_j^{-2}=\\|K_h\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-tA_h}=1+\\sum_{j\\ge1}e^{-t\\nu_j/2}<\\infty\\quad(t>0).$$ The heat series converges locally uniformly on $(0,\\infty)$. The ordered eigenvalues and this $C_{\\mathrm{loc}}((0,\\infty))$ heat trace have Borel versions as functions of the area measure in the fixed sphere coordinate. The form and these readouts are invariant under conformal or anticonformal changes of that coordinate.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tour-order",
        "exact_quote": "There is a measurable, measure-preserving map $\\tau:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$, defined up to a null set, such that $$\\tau_n(t)\\longrightarrow\\tau(t),\\qquad\n \\eta_2(\\tau(t))=\\eta_1(t)\n \\quad\\text{for almost every }t.$$ If intervals $J_n\\subset[0,1]$ have endpoints converging to the endpoints of an interval $J$, then $$\\mathbf 1_{J_n}\\longrightarrow\\mathbf 1_J,\\qquad\n \\mathbf 1_{J_n}\\circ\\tau_n\\longrightarrow\\mathbf 1_J\\circ\\tau\n \\quad\\text{in }H.$$ Finite products and differences of such indicator functions converge in $H$ as well. For either $r$, the map $$U_r:L^2(\\mu)\\longrightarrow H,\\qquad U_rg=g\\circ\\eta_r,$$ is unitary.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tour-order",
          "exact_quote": "There is a measurable, measure-preserving map $\\tau:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$, defined up to a null set, such that $$\\tau_n(t)\\longrightarrow\\tau(t),\\qquad\n \\eta_2(\\tau(t))=\\eta_1(t)\n \\quad\\text{for almost every }t.$$ If intervals $J_n\\subset[0,1]$ have endpoints converging to the endpoints of an interval $J$, then $$\\mathbf 1_{J_n}\\longrightarrow\\mathbf 1_J,\\qquad\n \\mathbf 1_{J_n}\\circ\\tau_n\\longrightarrow\\mathbf 1_J\\circ\\tau\n \\quad\\text{in }H.$$ Finite products and differences of such indicator functions converge in $H$ as well.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "On the present extraction there are constants $C_0,C_1<\\infty$ and linear maps $Q_{n,j}:\\mathcal H_n\\to H$, for $n\\ge n_0$ and $j\\ge J$, with $$\\label{eq:finite-rank-approximation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}Q_{n,j}\\le C_0 2^j,\\qquad\n \\lVert F-Q_{n,j}F\\rVert_H^2\n \\le C_1 2^{-pj}\\mathcal E_n^0(F),\\quad F\\in \\mathcal H_n.$$ For each fixed $j\\ge J$, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ have bounded norms, the sequence $Q_{n_q,j}F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. Consequently, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $$\\sup_q\\bigl(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathcal E_{n_q}^0(F_q)\\bigr)<\\infty,$$ the sequence $F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. There also exist a finite $i_0$ and a constant $c_*>0$ such that, for all $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$, $$\\label{eq:raw-eigenvalue-tail}\n \\nu_{n,i}\\ge c_* i^p.$$ The convention $\\nu_{n,i}=+\\infty$ for $i\\ge\\dim \\mathcal H_n$ is used here.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "On the present extraction there are constants $C_0,C_1<\\infty$ and linear maps $Q_{n,j}:\\mathcal H_n\\to H$, for $n\\ge n_0$ and $j\\ge J$, with $$\\label{eq:finite-rank-approximation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}Q_{n,j}\\le C_0 2^j,\\qquad\n \\lVert F-Q_{n,j}F\\rVert_H^2\n \\le C_1 2^{-pj}\\mathcal E_n^0(F),\\quad F\\in \\mathcal H_n.$$ For each fixed $j\\ge J$, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ have bounded norms, the sequence $Q_{n_q,j}F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. Consequently, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $$\\sup_q\\bigl(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathcal E_{n_q}^0(F_q)\\bigr)<\\infty,$$ the sequence $F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. There also exist a finite $i_0$ and a constant $c_*>0$ such that, for all $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$, $$\\label{eq:raw-eigenvalue-tail}\n \\nu_{n,i}\\ge c_* i^p.$$ The convention $\\nu_{n,i}=+\\infty$ for $i\\ge\\dim \\mathcal H_n$ is used here.",

      "exact_statement": "On the present extraction there are constants $C_0,C_1<\\infty$ and linear maps $Q_{n,j}:\\mathcal H_n\\to H$, for $n\\ge n_0$ and $j\\ge J$, with $$\\label{eq:finite-rank-approximation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}Q_{n,j}\\le C_0 2^j,\\qquad\n \\lVert F-Q_{n,j}F\\rVert_H^2\n \\le C_1 2^{-pj}\\mathcal E_n^0(F),\\quad F\\in \\mathcal H_n.$$ For each fixed $j\\ge J$, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ have bounded norms, the sequence $Q_{n_q,j}F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. Consequently, whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in\\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $$\\sup_q\\bigl(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathcal E_{n_q}^0(F_q)\\bigr)<\\infty,$$ the sequence $F_q$ has a strongly convergent subsequence in $H$. There also exist a finite $i_0$ and a constant $c_*>0$ such that, for all $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$, $$\\label{eq:raw-eigenvalue-tail}\n \\nu_{n,i}\\ge c_* i^p.$$ The convention $\\nu_{n,i}=+\\infty$ for $i\\ge\\dim \\mathcal H_n$ is used here.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H=L^2([0,1])$, and let $\\mathcal H_n\\subset H$ be finite-dimensional subspaces containing the constants. Let $B_n$ be nonnegative self-adjoint operators on $\\mathcal H_n$, with kernel exactly the constants. Write $\\mathfrak e_n(F)=\\langle F,B_nF\\rangle_H$, list their eigenvalues as $0=\\nu_{n,0}<\\nu_{n,1}\\le\\cdots$, and pad this list by $+\\infty$ past $\\dim \\mathcal H_n-1$. Let $K_n$ be the inverse of $B_n$ on $\\mathcal H_n\\cap\\mathbf 1^\\perp$, extended by zero on constants and on $\\mathcal H_n^\\perp$, and let $k_n\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ be its kernel. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in \\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $\\sup_q(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathfrak e_{n_q}(F_q))<\\infty$, some subsequence of $F_q$ converges strongly in $H$;*\n\n2.  *for some $p>1/2$, $c>0$, and finite $i_0,n_0$, one has $\\nu_{n,i}\\ge c i^p$ for every $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$;*\n\n3.  *there is a doubly centered $k\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ for which $\\mathfrak Dk_n\\to\\mathfrak Dk$ almost everywhere on $[0,1]^4$.*\n\n*Then the operator norms $\\lVert K_n\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}$ are bounded for large $n$, and $k_n\\to k$ in $L^2([0,1]^2)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H=L^2([0,1])$, and let $\\mathcal H_n\\subset H$ be finite-dimensional subspaces containing the constants. Let $B_n$ be nonnegative self-adjoint operators on $\\mathcal H_n$, with kernel exactly the constants. Write $\\mathfrak e_n(F)=\\langle F,B_nF\\rangle_H$, list their eigenvalues as $0=\\nu_{n,0}<\\nu_{n,1}\\le\\cdots$, and pad this list by $+\\infty$ past $\\dim \\mathcal H_n-1$. Let $K_n$ be the inverse of $B_n$ on $\\mathcal H_n\\cap\\mathbf 1^\\perp$, extended by zero on constants and on $\\mathcal H_n^\\perp$, and let $k_n\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ be its kernel. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in \\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $\\sup_q(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathfrak e_{n_q}(F_q))<\\infty$, some subsequence of $F_q$ converges strongly in $H$;*\n\n2.  *for some $p>1/2$, $c>0$, and finite $i_0,n_0$, one has $\\nu_{n,i}\\ge c i^p$ for every $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$;*\n\n3.  *there is a doubly centered $k\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ for which $\\mathfrak Dk_n\\to\\mathfrak Dk$ almost everywhere on $[0,1]^4$.*\n\n*Then the operator norms $\\lVert K_n\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}$ are bounded for large $n$, and $k_n\\to k$ in $L^2([0,1]^2)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H=L^2([0,1])$, and let $\\mathcal H_n\\subset H$ be finite-dimensional subspaces containing the constants. Let $B_n$ be nonnegative self-adjoint operators on $\\mathcal H_n$, with kernel exactly the constants. Write $\\mathfrak e_n(F)=\\langle F,B_nF\\rangle_H$, list their eigenvalues as $0=\\nu_{n,0}<\\nu_{n,1}\\le\\cdots$, and pad this list by $+\\infty$ past $\\dim \\mathcal H_n-1$. Let $K_n$ be the inverse of $B_n$ on $\\mathcal H_n\\cap\\mathbf 1^\\perp$, extended by zero on constants and on $\\mathcal H_n^\\perp$, and let $k_n\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ be its kernel. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *whenever $n_q\\to\\infty$ and $F_q\\in \\mathcal H_{n_q}$ satisfy $\\sup_q(\\lVert F_q\\rVert_H^2+\\mathfrak e_{n_q}(F_q))<\\infty$, some subsequence of $F_q$ converges strongly in $H$;*\n\n2.  *for some $p>1/2$, $c>0$, and finite $i_0,n_0$, one has $\\nu_{n,i}\\ge c i^p$ for every $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$;*\n\n3.  *there is a doubly centered $k\\in L^2([0,1]^2)$ for which $\\mathfrak Dk_n\\to\\mathfrak Dk$ almost everywhere on $[0,1]^4$.*\n\n*Then the operator norms $\\lVert K_n\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}$ are bounded for large $n$, and $k_n\\to k$ in $L^2([0,1]^2)$.",
      "explanation": "Compactness, an eigenvalue tail and four-point convergence give bounded inverses converging in L2 kernel norm.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H=L^2([0,1])$, and let $\\mathcal H_n\\subset H$ be finite-dimensional subspaces containing the constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-inverse-kernel-compactness",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "for every $n\\ge n_0$ and $i\\ge i_0$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-211.2",
  "schema": "pm.claims.v2"
}

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **corner-measure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $V_n=V(M_n)$, let $d_n$ be graph distance using every original map edge with length one, and put $$\label{eq:corner-measure} \mu_n=\frac1{2n}\sum_{v\in V_n}\deg(v)\delta_v .$$ A loop contributes two to the degree. Thus $\mu_n$ is normalized corner counting pushed to vertices.
- **walk-generator** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The walk waits an exponential time of mean one, chooses an incident half-edge $a$ uniformly, and moves to its opposite endpoint $\operatorname{opp}(a)$.
- **continuum-metric-measure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $\mu_h$ for its quantum area probability measure and $D_h$ for its intrinsic LQG metric in one fixed deterministic normalization.
- **diameter-center** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a finite real random variable $Y$, let $\mathfrak m(Y)$ be the unique $r$ for which $\mathbb E[\arctan(Y-r)]=0$.
- **walk-traversal-count** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a finite walk $\pi$, let $N_e(\pi)$ be the number of traversals of $e$, in either direction and with multiplicity.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalization): summation by parts over half-edges -> walk operator is the raw energy operator
- **step-geometry** [claim](#stmt-prop-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-geometry): companion limits and tightness -> common coupling
- **step-readout** [claim](#stmt-prop-readout-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-readout-extension): conditional-law resampling -> local observation kernels
- **step-network** [claim](#stmt-thm-network-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edge-quantiles-2): primal-dual obstruction -> electrical bounds
- **step-conductivity** [claim](#stmt-thm-conductivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-square-conductance-2): relaxation and self-duality -> unit conductivity
- **step-four-point** [claim](#stmt-thm-four-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-energy-defect-2): local minimization and flux -> voltage limit
- **step-block** [claim](#stmt-thm-block-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flow-availability): address estimates and circuit covers -> block approximation
- **step-continuum** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuum-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuum-form): deterministic trace argument -> Dirichlet form and Green operator
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inverse-kernel-compactness-2): Hilbert-Schmidt convergence -> joint limit

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-energy-normalization**: For a finite connected planar multigraph, the scaled walk generator B_n is the diagonal-inverse-corner-measure times the unit-conductance graph Laplacian, and its quadratic form on L^2(mu_n) is the raw edge energy counting every parallel edge once and every loop as zero.
- **inv-constant-kernel**: The kernel of B_n is exactly the constant functions, so every row of the matrix of B_n sums to zero.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-disconnected-map**: Apply the kernel statement to a disconnected graph or a graph given as several components. -> Zero-energy functions are then constant per component, so the kernel is larger than the constants.
- **anti-off-critical-parameter**: Read the finite matrix identity as evidence about the limiting Liouville parameter for other cluster weights. -> The identity is purely algebraic and says nothing about which continuum limit arises; the limit parameter is tied to the FK-Ising weight.
- **anti-halved-convention**: Mix the halved Brownian energy with the raw unhalved electrical energy when comparing spectra. -> Eigenvalues differ by a factor of two and the clock is mis-scaled.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Lemma 2 is an explicit finite statement: for a given finite multigraph the scaled generator matrix is a computable rational matrix and the stated identities can be checked exactly.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens: V n, then n pairs u v (0-based endpoints; loops and parallel edges allowed)
# PRECONDITIONS: V>=1, n>=1, graph connected, all endpoints in range
# OUTPUTS: V lines, each with V exact rationals, the matrix of B=-2nL in vertex coordinates
# TIME: O(V^2 + n)
# SPACE: O(V^2)
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic via Fraction
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    V=int(data[0]); n=int(data[1])
    half=[[] for _ in range(V)]
    for i in range(n):
        u=int(data[2+2*i]); v=int(data[3+2*i])
        half[u].append(v)
        half[v].append(u)
    out=[]
    for v in range(V):
        d=len(half[v])
        row=[Fraction(0)]*V
        for w in half[v]:
            row[w]+=Fraction(1,d)
        row[v]-=1
        out.append(' '.join(str(-2*n*x) for x in row))
    print('\n'.join(out))
main()
```

| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no protocol, message model or fault model. The random walk is a mathematical Markov process on a single map. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is pure mathematics, in probability, statistical mechanics and Liouville quantum gravity. It identifies the limiting spectrum and heat trace but yields no engineering construction, so the contribution is purely theoretical. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-connected-map** (hypothesis): The identification of the kernel of the scaled walk operator with the constants, and the energy-to-operator identity, use that the map is connected; on a disconnected graph zero-energy functions are only constant on each component. Blocked use: ['Asserting a one-dimensional kernel for a graph given as several components', 'Applying the normalization lemma to a map that is not connected']
- **lim-loops-and-multiplicities** (edge_case): The model keeps loops and multiple edges as distinct occurrences: a loop adds to the degree but contributes no energy, and each parallel edge is its own summand. Computations that simplify the multigraph change the corner measure and the energy. Blocked use: ['Replacing the map by its underlying simple graph before computing the corner measure or the energy', 'Counting a loop as a single half-edge when forming degrees']
- **lim-critical-fk-parameter** (formal_scope): The identification of the limit as Liouville Brownian motion on the quantum sphere is tied to the FK-Ising weight and its Liouville parameter; the finite algebraic identities carry no information about other cluster weights. Blocked use: ['Transferring the spectral limit and its clock to other cluster weights', 'Reading the limit parameter off the finite normalization identity']
- **lim-subsequence-extraction** (nonconstructive): The geometric coupling is obtained only after passing to a further subsequence of any given sequence, with the full statement recovered by a subsequence criterion for convergence in distribution; no explicit construction or rate is supplied. Blocked use: ['Presenting the almost-sure coupling as a statement about the original sequence', 'Extracting a convergence rate or a finite-size error bound from the extraction']
- **lim-sample-dependent-block-constants** (approximation): The block approximation holds with finite constants and thresholds that depend on the sample and on the activated atlas entry, so the estimate is qualitative in its constants even though the exponent is explicit. Blocked use: ['Quoting a deterministic constant or a deterministic starting level for the block approximation', 'Using the estimate as a quantitative finite-size guarantee']
- **lim-random-ball-constants** (approximation): The polynomial small-ball bounds for the quantum area hold only with random constants and a random radius threshold that depend on the chosen coordinate; they are not effective bounds. Blocked use: ['Using explicit numerical constants in the small-ball estimate', 'Applying the bound uniformly over an ensemble of surfaces']
- **lim-activation-default-value** (edge_case): Local observations are controlled only on the activation event; outside it the observation may carry an arbitrary fixed value and nothing is asserted about it. Blocked use: ['Using observation values on non-activated charts as data', 'Conditioning a finite word on success of a numerical observation']
- **lim-fixed-test-thresholds** (hypothesis): The electrical bounds hold for fixed activated tests only, with an eventual index depending on the test and the sample; they do not give a threshold uniform over scales, which is why a separate all-scale block estimate is needed. Blocked use: ['Applying the annular bounds at mesh-dependent scales without a fixed test', 'Treating the fixed-test constants as a uniform-in-scale statement']
- **lim-duality-fixed-point-order** (formal_scope): The unit conductivity follows from the self-duality fixed-point idea only after existence, determinism and equality of the primal and dual scalar limits have been proved; the finite product-one identity alone does not fix the scalar. Blocked use: ['Deriving unit conductivity from the finite conductance product without establishing a deterministic isotropic scalar', 'Applying the self-duality argument to a network whose primal and dual scalars are not shown equal']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Using the conformal and metric-measure companion results and the stated Brownian/Liouville quantum gravity inputs, we prove spectral convergence for critical spherical FK–Ising maps to Liouville Brownian motion on the ordinary unit-area $`\sqrt3`$-quantum sphere. The discrete walk has total attempt rate one, uses every map edge including loops and multiplicities, and has the corner measure as its stationary law. Accelerating time by the number of map edges gives joint convergence of the metric-measure space, all ordered eigenvalues with multiplicities and padding, and the heat trace locally uniformly at strictly positive times. The conductivity and clock constant are one in these conventions.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Random planar maps provide discrete random surfaces on which geometry and statistical mechanics interact. The random-cluster formulation of Fortuin and Kasteleyn includes the Ising model at cluster weight two (Fortuin and Kasteleyn 1972) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[FortuinKasteleyn1972RandomCluster](#ref-FortuinKasteleyn1972RandomCluster)]. For maps decorated by the critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn (FK) model, Sheffield’s inventory accumulation bijection gives an exact word encoding (Sheffield 2016) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Sheffield2016Inventory](#ref-Sheffield2016Inventory)]. Its spanning-tree specialization is the correspondence of Mullin studied by Bernardi, and Sheffield identifies its fixed-map construction with Bernardi’s subgraph bijection (Mullin 1967; Bernardi 2007, 2008; Sheffield 2016) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Mullin1967TreeRooted](#ref-Mullin1967TreeRooted), [Bernardi2007TreeRooted](#ref-Bernardi2007TreeRooted), [Bernardi2008Subgraphs](#ref-Bernardi2008Subgraphs), [Sheffield2016Inventory](#ref-Sheffield2016Inventory)]. Sheffield also relates the infinite-volume inventory contours to correlated Brownian motion (Sheffield 2016, Theorem 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Sheffield2016Inventory](#ref-Sheffield2016Inventory)]. The cone-time, local, and finite-volume refinements of Gwynne, Mao, and Sun and of Gwynne and Sun identify further contour and loop data (Gwynne, Mao, and Sun 2019; Gwynne and Sun 2017, 2015) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GwynneMaoSun2019ConeTimes](#ref-GwynneMaoSun2019ConeTimes), [GwynneSun2017Local](#ref-GwynneSun2017Local), [GwynneSun2015Finite](#ref-GwynneSun2015Finite)]. The mating-of-trees theory of Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield, and its finite-sphere form due to Miller and Sheffield, identify the corresponding Brownian sewing with a Liouville quantum gravity (LQG) surface explored by space-filling Schramm–Loewner evolution (Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield 2021; Miller and Sheffield 2019) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating](#ref-DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating), [MillerSheffield2019SphereMating](#ref-MillerSheffield2019SphereMating)]. For the FK–Ising parameter $q=2$, the resulting LQG parameter is $\gamma=\sqrt3$. The Brownian paths in this encoding record the two boundary-length contours; the vertex walk studied here is a separate stochastic process on the map.

The continuum surface carries several related structures. Its quantum area comes from Gaussian multiplicative chaos (GMC) in the LQG construction of Duplantier and Sheffield (Duplantier and Sheffield 2011) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DuplantierSheffield2011KPZ](#ref-DuplantierSheffield2011KPZ)]. The construction of its intrinsic metric proceeds through Liouville first-passage percolation: Ding, Dubédat, Dunlap, and Falconet proved the required tightness, and Gwynne and Miller proved existence, uniqueness up to a deterministic constant, and conformal covariance of the LQG metric (Ding et al. 2020; Gwynne and Miller 2021b, 2021a) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DingDubedatDunlapFalconet2020Tightness](#ref-DingDubedatDunlapFalconet2020Tightness), [GwynneMiller2021Metric](#ref-GwynneMiller2021Metric), [GwynneMiller2021ConformalMetric](#ref-GwynneMiller2021ConformalMetric)]. For discrete conformal coordinates, Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield proved a joint area and exploration limit for the Tutte embedding of disk mated-CRT maps, including a Brownian walk limit modulo time parameterization (Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield 2021) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GwynneMillerSheffield2021Tutte](#ref-GwynneMillerSheffield2021Tutte)]. Holden and Sun proved joint embedded metric and measure convergence for critical Boltzmann triangulations of polygons under the Cardy embedding (Holden and Sun 2023) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HoldenSun2023Cardy](#ref-HoldenSun2023Cardy)]. These results illustrate the importance of keeping metric, measure, and conformal coordinates coupled.

Liouville Brownian motion provides the diffusion associated with conformal Brownian energy and quantum area. Constructions were given by Berestycki and by Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas (Berestycki 2015; Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas 2016) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Berestycki2015Diffusion](#ref-Berestycki2015Diffusion), [GarbanRhodesVargas2016LBM](#ref-GarbanRhodesVargas2016LBM)]. The Dirichlet-form description and heat-kernel theory were developed further by Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas, by Andres and Kajino, and by Maillard, Rhodes, Vargas, and Zeitouni (Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas 2014; Andres and Kajino 2016; Maillard et al. 2016) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GarbanRhodesVargas2014Dirichlet](#ref-GarbanRhodesVargas2014Dirichlet), [AndresKajino2016Heat](#ref-AndresKajino2016Heat), [MaillardRhodesVargasZeitouni2016Heat](#ref-MaillardRhodesVargasZeitouni2016Heat)]. In particular, the centered logarithmic Green operator gives a compact spectral description in the torus setting of the last work. Passing to the ordinary unit-area quantum sphere requires a local comparison of its field law with these Gaussian reference models.

For discrete walks, Berestycki and Gwynne proved convergence of walks on mated-CRT maps to Liouville Brownian motion in whole-plane and disk settings, with the limiting clock given a fixed annealed median exit normalization (Berestycki and Gwynne 2022, Equation (1.6) and Theorems 1.2 and 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM](#ref-BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM)]. Their deterministic discrete time normalization $m_\varepsilon$ is defined by an annealed median exit time and satisfies $m_\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon^{-1}$; their Remark 1.3 leaves convergence of $\varepsilon m_\varepsilon$ unresolved. Electrical estimates for mated-CRT maps were established by Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield (Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield 2019) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[GwynneMillerSheffield2019Harmonic](#ref-GwynneMillerSheffield2019Harmonic)], while Gwynne and Miller obtained resistance, displacement, and spectral-dimension estimates for several infinite planar-map ensembles (Gwynne and Miller 2021c) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GwynneMiller2021RandomWalk](#ref-GwynneMiller2021RandomWalk)]. To pass to a finite spectrum and heat trace, one must control the spatial diffusion, its time normalization, and the spectral mass contributing at positive times.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model and the theorem

For $n\ge1$, let $\mathcal F_n$ be the finite set of pairs $(M,A)$, where $M$ is a connected planar multigraph with $n$ edges embedded in the oriented sphere and rooted at an oriented edge, and $A\subseteq E(M)$. Equivalence is by orientation-preserving homeomorphisms preserving the root and $A$. Loops and multiple edges are allowed. Let $M^*$ be the dual map and $A^*=\{e^*:e\notin A\}$. Write $k(A)$ for the number of components of the spanning subgraph $(V(M),A)$, including isolated vertices, and set $$\ell(M,A)=k(A)+k(A^*)-1=2k(A)+|A|-|V(M)|.$$ The second identity is Euler’s formula applied to the embedded spanning subgraph. The critical FK–Ising law is <a id="eq:fk-law"></a>$$\label{eq:fk-law}
 \mathbb P\bigl((M_n,A_n)=(M,A)\bigr)=\frac{2^{\ell(M,A)/2}}{Z_n},
 \qquad
 Z_n=\sum_{(M,A)\in\mathcal F_n}2^{\ell(M,A)/2}.$$ Let $V_n=V(M_n)$, let $d_n$ be graph distance using every original map edge with length one, and put <a id="eq:corner-measure"></a>$$\label{eq:corner-measure}
 \mu_n=\frac1{2n}\sum_{v\in V_n}\deg(v)\delta_v .$$ A loop contributes two to the degree. Thus $\mu_n$ is normalized corner counting pushed to vertices.

The walk waits an exponential time of mean one, chooses an incident half-edge $a$ uniformly, and moves to its opposite endpoint $\operatorname{opp}(a)$. A chosen loop leaves the position unchanged. Its generator is <a id="eq:walk-generator"></a>$$\label{eq:walk-generator}
 (L_nf)(v)=\frac1{\deg(v)}
 \sum_{\substack{a\ \mathrm{half\text{-}edge}\\ a\ \mathrm{incident\ to}\ v}}
 \bigl(f(\operatorname{opp}(a))-f(v)\bigr).$$ The operator $-L_n$ is nonnegative and self-adjoint on $L^2(\mu_n)$. List its eigenvalues with multiplicity as $$0=\lambda_{n,0}\le\lambda_{n,1}\le\cdots
 \le\lambda_{n,|V_n|-1},$$ and put $\lambda_{n,j}=+\infty$ for $j\ge |V_n|$. For $t>0$, define <a id="eq:discrete-heat-trace"></a>$$\label{eq:discrete-heat-trace}
 H_n(t)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{ntL_n}
       =\sum_{j=0}^{|V_n|-1}e^{-nt\lambda_{n,j}}.$$ This is the operator trace, with each mode counted once.

<a id="eq:continuum-energy"></a>
For the continuum object, let $(\mathbb S^2,h)$ be the ordinary unit-area $\gamma$-Liouville quantum gravity sphere at $\gamma=\sqrt3$: the ordinary quantum-sphere measure of Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield, disintegrated at quantum area one, with its original marks forgotten (Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield 2021, Definition 4.21(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating](#ref-DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating)]. Write $\mu_h$ for its quantum area probability measure and $D_h$ for its intrinsic LQG metric in one fixed deterministic normalization. We use the conformal coordinate of the round sphere. On smooth functions define $$\label{eq:continuum-energy}
 \mathcal E_h(f,g)=\frac12\int_{\mathbb S^2}
       \langle \nabla f,\nabla g\rangle_{\mathrm{round}}\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}_{\mathrm{round}}.$$ The proof will establish that its closure on $L^2(\mu_h)$, using the usual quasi-continuous Sobolev representatives, is the Liouville Brownian motion form and has compact resolvent. Let $A_h$ be its nonnegative operator and $$0=\Lambda_0(h)\le\Lambda_1(h)\le\Lambda_2(h)\le\cdots$$ its eigenvalues with multiplicity. Set <a id="eq:continuum-heat-trace"></a>$$\label{eq:continuum-heat-trace}
 H_h(t)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-tA_h}=\sum_{j\ge0}e^{-t\Lambda_j(h)},\qquad t>0.$$ The factor $1/2$ in <a href="#eq:continuum-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuum-energy">[eq:continuum-energy]</a> fixes the Brownian clock. The two-dimensional Dirichlet integral is conformally invariant. Pushing $\mu_h$ forward under a change of conformal coordinate therefore gives a unitarily equivalent operator.

<a id="eq:distance-normalization"></a>
Here is the deterministic normalization of space. For a finite real random variable $Y$, let $\mathfrak m(Y)$ be the unique $r$ for which $\mathbb E[\arctan(Y-r)]=0$. Put $$\Delta_n=\max\{1,\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(V_n,d_n)\},\qquad
 \Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(\mathbb S^2,D_h),$$ and define $$\label{eq:distance-normalization}
 a_n=\exp\{\mathfrak m(\log\Delta)-\mathfrak m(\log\Delta_n)\}.$$ Both centers use unconditional laws. In particular $a_n$ is deterministic.
